### msg:10075
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/10075:1#76ac2430

```
Note: the Windows profile for %1 could not be removed yet, and SD has kept it.\n\nWindows still has the profile's registry hive mounted, which locks a file\ninside the directory, and nothing can delete OR rename it until that is\nreleased. Only a restart releases it.\n\nSD has kept the profile's registry entry with the directory rather than\nremoving one half of a pair, and has recorded both. THE NEXT TIME THIS\nCOMPUTER STARTS, the SD service removes them; nothing else has to be done.\n\nUntil then the name is taken. An account created as %1 before that restart\nwill NOT get the old home back: Windows will not put a new profile where one\nalready sits, and gives it a suffixed home instead, so anything kept in the\nold one - ssh keys included - will not be found there. CREATE.ACCOUNT refuses\nrather than let that happen quietly.
```

cited by: `delacc`

### msg:10089
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/10089:1#7ffd6187

```
Account %1 needs a password.\n\nWhen you sign in to this computer at the keyboard you need only your\nWindows password.  When you connect from another computer, over ssh or\nthrough the SD API, you need only this password.\n\nYou will be asked to type it twice.  What you type is not shown - each\ncharacter appears as a star - and Backspace corrects a mistake.\n\nA password is required.  Pressing Enter on an empty line does not give\nyou an account without one - it ends this session, and you are asked\nagain the next time you sign in.  Until this account has a password it\ncannot be reached from another computer: not over ssh, and not through\nthe SD API.  It still works here at the keyboard.
```

### msg:10116
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/10116:1#8b285a4e

```
Note: the Windows profile registry entry for %1 could not be removed yet.\n\nThe profile DIRECTORY is gone. Windows still holds the registry record of it,\nand if an account called %1 is created again before that record clears,\nWindows honors it and gives the account a DIFFERENT home directory -\nC:\Users\%1 with a suffix added - so anything expected in the old one, ssh\nkeys included, will not be found there.\n\nSD has recorded it. THE NEXT TIME THIS COMPUTER STARTS, the SD service\nremoves the record; nothing else has to be done. CREATE.ACCOUNT refuses the\nname in the meantime rather than let the wrong home be assigned quietly.\n\nAn administrator can also clear it now: "Get-CimInstance Win32_UserProfile"\nfrom an elevated prompt lists the orphaned profile, and "Remove-CimInstance"\non the entry whose LocalPath is the old home directory removes it.
```

cited by: `delacc`

### msg:10123
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/10123:1#ab22ed87

```
WARNING: the Windows profile for %1 was left behind and nothing will remove it.\n\nWindows still has the profile's registry hive mounted, which locks a file\ninside the directory, so neither the directory nor its registry entry could be\nremoved. SD keeps the two together in that case and records them so that the\nservice can clear them at the next restart - AND THE RECORD COULD NOT BE\nWRITTEN, so that will not happen.\n\nThis one needs a person. Restart this computer, then, from an elevated prompt,\ndelete C:\Users\%1 and remove the profile that "Get-CimInstance\nWin32_UserProfile" still lists for it.\n\nUntil that is done the name is taken: an account created as %1 gets a suffixed\nhome and will not find anything kept in the old one, ssh keys included.\n\nC:\ProgramData\SD\profile-reclaim is where the record should have gone. If it\nis missing, or SD cannot write to it, that is what to repair.
```

cited by: `delacc`

### msg:10124
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/10124:1#1487ead5

```
Cannot create %1: a Windows profile directory is already there.\n\n    %2\n\nWindows will not put a new profile where one already sits. It would give %1 a\nsuffixed home instead - the same name with the computer name added - and\nanything expected in the directory above, ssh keys included, would not be\nfound there. That is a wrong answer nothing says out loud, so this is refused\ninstead.\n\nIt is almost always the profile of an account of this name that was deleted\nwhile Windows still had its registry hive mounted. SD cannot clear it from\nhere - the hive is still up, and only a restart releases it.\n\nIf SD deleted that account, it recorded the directory and the SD service\nremoves it at the next restart of this computer: restart, then create the\naccount. Otherwise delete the directory from an elevated prompt first.
```

### msg:10125
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/10125:1#f6b01f5a

```
Cannot create %1: the check for a leftover Windows profile did not run (%2).\n\nBefore creating an account SD tests whether a profile directory of that name\nis already sitting under C:\Users. If one is, Windows silently gives the new\naccount a suffixed home and anything expected in the old directory - ssh keys\nincluded - is not found there.\n\nThat test could not be made, so this creation is refused rather than made\nblind. It is the one thing here that cannot be put right afterwards.\n\nStatus 1 means PowerShell could not be run at all; status 2 means the account\nname was rejected before anything was measured.
```

### msg:10130
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/10130:1#8cd9b3f8

```
The Windows account %1 was created, and removing it again FAILED - so it is still on this computer.\n\nNothing was created on the SD side: no account record, no VOC and no sdu_ group,\nand the account is in none of SD's groups. So SD cannot see it, delete.account\ncannot remove it, and the sweep an uninstall offers will not find it either -\nthat sweep works from membership of sdusers, which this account never joined.\n\nIt is also NOT confined. Accounts SD finishes creating are put in sdsshonly,\nwhich denies the console and Remote Desktop; this one is not, so it can sign in\nto Windows like any ordinary account, with the password that was set for it.\n\nRemove it with Windows' own tools, or run create.account for the same name from\nan ELEVATED session, which will take it over and finish the job properly.
```

```
remote.api on | local | off\n\nON     SD listens on port 4247 and other computers may connect.\nLOCAL  SD listens on port 4247 and only this computer may connect.\nOFF    SD opens no API socket at all.\n\nWith no keyword it reports the current setting and changes nothing.\n\nON and OFF change whether SD opens a socket, which it decides when it starts,\nso they offer to restart SD. LOCAL and ON differ only in the firewall and take\neffect at once.
```

### msg:10148

```
Removal accepted, and it completes when Windows next restarts.\n\nUNTIL YOU RESTART, THE ssh SERVER IS STILL HERE AND STILL RUNNING. Windows\nstages the removal rather than doing it at once, so ssh going on working now is\nexpected and is not a fault.\n\nC:\ProgramData\ssh is left in place. It holds the host keys and sshd_config,\nand Windows does not remove it with the capability. That matters for ONE thing:\nrunning the SD INSTALLER on this machine again. Setup compares sshd_config\nagainst the copy Windows ships, sshd_config_default, and that copy went with the\ncapability, so Setup cannot tell whether the configuration has been edited and\nstops rather than guess. "ssh.server install" is NOT affected: it puts the\nserver back, and sshd_config_default comes back with it. Remove that directory\ntoo if you do not intend to run an ssh server on this machine again.
```

```
User %1 was not running, so there was nothing to log out.\n\nIts session slot has been reclaimed, and every file, record, group and task lock it still held has been released.  That is what you wanted: a session that dies without logging out leaves its locks behind, and until the slot is cleared nothing else can gain exclusive access to the files it had open.\n\nIf you were trying to reach a file that would not open, try again now.
```

If that session is one of your own, close the file there or leave it.  If it is a session that has DIED - an ssh connection that dropped, a window that was closed - its slot is still holding the file, and clearing it is what releases the file:\n\n   logout <user number>\n\nThat reclaims a session whose process is gone.  If the process is still running it is asked to log out instead, so check with the person first.

```
The OpenSSH server was removed. It is gone now and no restart is needed.\n\nC:\ProgramData\ssh is left in place. It holds the host keys and sshd_config,\nand the OpenSSH uninstaller does not remove it. That matters for ONE thing:\nrunning the SD INSTALLER on this machine again. Setup compares sshd_config\nagainst the copy the server ships, sshd_config_default, and that copy went with\nthe server, so Setup cannot tell whether the configuration has been edited and\nstops rather than guess. "ssh.server install" is NOT affected: it puts the\nserver back, and sshd_config_default comes back with it. Remove that directory\ntoo if you do not intend to run an ssh server on this machine again.
```

cited by: `sshsrvr`

### msg:12219
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/12219:1#3a9cac50

```
The IDE\n\nTab, Shift-Tab   In the editor: indent, outdent the line or the selected lines (3 blanks, one undo step); Tab does not move between the panes\nF6, Shift-F6     Next / previous pane (the same keys move between the boxes of every page)\nReturn           Open the file or record; go to the error\nSpace, Right     Open or close a tree node\nCtrl-S           Save the record (an untitled one asks for its file and name; path:/full/name there makes a file of the operating system)\nCtrl-N           New record (path:/full/name makes an empty file of the operating system and opens it)\nCtrl-O           Open a record by file and name; path:/full/name there opens an operating system file\nCtrl-W           Close the record\nCtrl-PgDn, PgUp  Next / previous record tab (Alt-PgDn, PgUp too: a terminal may keep the Ctrl keys for its own tabs)\n
```

### msg:12220
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/12220:1#43a9ea61

```
F4               Next error or result\nF5, Ctrl-L       Draw the screen again (as on every page)\nF9               Run\nF7              Debug (it runs on to the first breakpoint mark; Build / Debug from the first line stops at the first line)\nShift-F7         Set or clear the breakpoint mark of the line (a click in the gutter does the same; Build / Clear breakpoints)\nF8               Compile\nShift-F9         Build: compile and catalog\nAlt-G            Go to a line\nCtrl-T           The next theme (kept for you; View, Theme)\nCtrl-K           The keys (Help, Keys)\nCtrl-E           Zoom: the editor fills the screen, or the files come back\nF12              Edit the record as fields: values and subvalues in CED (Ctrl-S there brings the fields back here)\nMarks            A dictionary item's, a VOC record's or a data record's value, subvalue and text marks show as :@vm: :@sm: :@tm: and are read back as marks (a program's never)\nLocks            A record open in a tab is locked; one another session has locked opens read-only\nNot ASCII        A record holding characters outside ASCII (bytes 128 to 247) opens read-only\nIdle             With autologout set, the status line counts down its last 60 seconds; any key stops it\nInclude          Go, Open the include: the record a $include line at the cursor names\nMenus            Save as, Write a copy and Find in the records of the file are in the File and Search menus\n
```

cited by: `ide`

### msg:12221
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/12221:1#73ea9654

```
Ctrl-F, Ctrl-B   Find forward, backward   F3, Shift-F3  The next, the previous (Return in the question finds the next)   Ctrl-R  Replace\nEsc, F10        The menus (Files and records is in the Go menu)\nCtrl-Q           Quit\nF1               This screen\nCtrl-A           Select all   Ctrl-D  Duplicate the line   Alt-K  Cut the line\nCtrl-P, Ctrl-U   A word back, on (Ctrl-Left, Right too)   Ctrl-Home, Ctrl-End  Text start, end   Ctrl-Del  Delete word   Ins  Overwrite\nAlt-R, Alt-P     Start or end a keyboard macro, play it\nFile menu        Spool the text prints it as it is now, saved or not: at printer, lnum, region\nSearch menu      List matching lines (the lines that hold a text, in the table), Query replace (Yes, No, All, Quit), Find options (C = match case, W = whole words), Upcase and Downcase the selection (Ctrl-G sets the mark), Import a record here, Export the selection to a record\nBuild menu       Run a command: what it prints is in the table. Run a command at the terminal: the screen is given to it, and Return comes back here. Format the program lays it out again, indenting by the tab width\nGo to line       A line; line,column; or field,value,subvalue in a record shown with :@vm: marks\nGo menu          Next record from the list: the next record of the select list the IDE started with, in a tab of its own. Filter the records: which records the tree lists for a file (abc..., or LIKE or WITH and a query)\nOpen (Ctrl-O)    Up, Down in the file or the record bring back the files and records opened this session\nView menu        Theme (Ctrl-T), Refresh (F5) draws the screen again; Overwrite mode, Auto-indent (Return keeps the indent), Tab width; List of tabs (Return goes to the tab)\nTools menu       The development pages: Files, Dictionary, Vocabulary, Queries, Bulk update, Programs, Tests. Leaving the page (Ctrl-Q) and pressing Return comes back here
```

```
COPY filename itemlist (options
To: (destination.filename destination.itemlist

itemlist: item ids desired to copy to destination file
          * for all items in the file
          If omitted, uses an active select list

options:  (D - Deletes source records after copying
          (I - Suppresses display of record id
          (N - No pagination when printing to screen
          (O - Overwrite destination items
          (P - Output to printer
          (S - Suppresses field numbers with P/T
          (T - Output to the terminal
```

```
.An text     Append text to command stack entry n.\n.Cn/s1/s2/G  Replace s1 by s2 in stack entry n. G = global replace.\n.Dn          Delete stack entry n.\n.D name      Delete named sentence or paragraph.\n.In text     Insert text as stack entry n.\n.Ln          Display last n lines from stack. Default is 20.\n.L name      Display named sentence or paragraph.\n.Rn          Recall stack entry n to top of stack.\n.R name      Recall named sentence or paragraph to stack.\n.S name s e  Save stack entries s to e as sentence or paragraph name.\n.Un          Convert stack entry n to uppercase\n.Xn          Execute command n. Default is 1.\n.X file id   Execute command stored in named file and record\nSpaces are required where shown.  n defaults to one in all cases if omitted.
```

### msg:6603
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6603:1#3a809d37

```
+n\tMove forward by n lines
-n\tMove backward by n lines
.A[n] string\tAppend string to entry n (default 1) of the command stack
.C[n]/old/new/\tChange old to new in line n default(1) of the command stack
.D[n]\tDelete line n (default 1) of the command stack
.I[n] string\tInsert string as entry n (default 1) of the command stack
.L[n]\tList n (default 9) lines of the command stack
.R[n]\tRecall line n (default 1) of the command stack to stack top
.X[n]\tExecute line n (default 1) of the command stack
<\tSet current line to be the start line of the block
<>\tSet block to be just the current line
>\tSet current line to be the end line of the block
?\tDisplay status information
A [string]\tAppend string to the current line. Repeats last if A omitted
B\tMove to the last line of the record
B string\tSplit current line into two after string
BLOCK\tToggle block verification mode
C/old.string/new.string/[n][G][B]\tChange old.string to new.string in n (default 1) lines from\xfbthe current line.  Valid delimiters are\xfb   ! ":'"':" # $ % & ( ) * + , - . / : = @ [ ] \ _ ` ' { } |\xfbG causes all occurrences of old.string to be replaced\xfbB applies the change to the currently defined block\xfbEntering C with no strings repeats the last substitution
CASE OFF\tSet case insensitive mode for the F and L commands
CASE ON\tSet case sensitive mode for the F and L commands
CAT [string]\tConcatenate current line, string and following line
COL\tDisplay a column number ruler to aid alignment of inserted text
COPY\tCopy currently defined block to after the current position
DELETE\tDelete entire record from the file, terminating edit
DE[]\tDelete n (default 1) lines starting at the current position
DROP\tDelete currently defined block
DUP [n]\tDuplicate the current line n (default 1) times
D[n]\tDelete n (default 1) lines starting at the current position
EV\tEnter EV mode to edit values on current line
FIle [[filename] record.id]\tWrite record and end edit
F[n] [string]\tFind next line containing string at column n (default 1)
FORMAT\tFormat SDBasic program source
G< \tMove to the first line of the currently defined block
G> \tMove to the last line of the currently defined block
Gn\tMove to line n
HELP [topic]\tDisplay short description associated with topic
I [text]\tInsert text after the current line
IB [text]\tInsert text before the current line
L [string]\tFind next line containing string (default as last L command)
Ln\tDisplay n lines, moving current line to final displayed line
LOAD [filename] record.id\tInsert part of specified record
LOOP [lineno [count]]\tRepeat from given line in prestored edit sequence
M [pattern]\tFind next line matching pattern
MOVE\t                Copy block to current position and delete original
NPRINT\tToggles non-printing character entry mode
n\tMove to line n
OOPS\tUndo most recent function that modified the record
PAUSE\tPause prestored edit sequence. Use .XR to resume, .XK to abort
PB\tDisplay currently defined block
PL[[-]n]\tDisplay n lines relative to the current line position
POn\tMove to line n
PP[n]\tDisplays n lines surrounding the current line position
P[n]\tDisplay n lines. Default n is as in last P command
Quit\tEnd edit without saving any changes made to the record
QV\tExit from EV (edit value) mode, discarding changes
R [text]\tReplace current line with the text
R/old.string/new.string/[n][G][B]\tChange old.string to new.string in n (default 1) lines from\xfbcurrent line\xfbDelimiter is ! "" # $ % & ( ) * + , - . / : = @ [ ] \ _ ` ' { } |\xfbG causes all occurrences of old.string to be replaced\xfbB applies the change to the currently defined block\xfbEntering C with no strings repeats the last substitution
SAve [[filename] record.id]\tWrite record
SV\tExit from EV (edit value) mode, saving changes
T\tMove to top (before line 1)
UNLOAD [[DICT] filename] record.id\tSave part of record in named file
X\tAbort edit when a select list is in use without saving changes
XEQ [command]\tExecute CPROC command
^\tToggle non-printing character expansion mode
```

cited by: `ed`

### msg:6612
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6612:1#92a5ae6e

```
The Title line
Every menu has a title to appear on the top line of the screen.  This title
will be centered in the display device width.  If the text is wider than the
display, excess characters are discarded.
```

### msg:6613
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6613:1#e8e5653c

```
The Access Control Subroutine
A menu can have an associated access control subroutine.  This allows access
to items on the menu to be restricted in any way the application designer
wishes.  Some options might, for example, be available only to certain users.

When an access control subroutine is specified, the subroutine is called for
each menu option as the menu is prepared for display, passing in the access
control key on the Access line of the menu option details.  The subroutine
returns a status indicating whether the menu item is available.
```

### msg:6614
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6614:1#eb2693d5

```
The Prompt Text
The prompt text appears under the menu item list, inviting the user to select
an option from the displayed menu.  The prompt is displayed exactly as entered,
with no trailing space.  If no prompt text is specified, a default prompt text
is used.
```

### msg:6615
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6615:1#158b85ca

```
Exit Codes
The optional Exits item is a comma separated list of case insensitive codes
that the user may enter at the menu prompt to exit from the menu, returning to
the process that called the menu (another menu, a paragraph, a program or the
command prompt).

If no exit codes are specified, entry of a null response to the prompt will
exit from the menu.
```

### msg:6616
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6616:1#d11cde9f

```
Stop Codes
The optional Stops item is a comma separated list of case insensitive codes
that the user may enter at the menu prompt to terminate the menu and all other
active programs, menus, paragraphs, etc.  An abort event is generated and any
ON.ABORT paragraph will be executed before returning to the command prompt.

If no stop codes are specified, entry of QUIT at the prompt will abort the
menu.
```

### msg:6617
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6617:1#2ea8d456

```
Menu Option Text
The Text item is the text for an individual menu option. The menu option number
will be added automatically.  If the text is wider than the display device
after the number has been added, excess characters are lost.

A menu with all text items short enough to display in half the width of the
display device will automatically wrap into two columns if there are more
options than can be displayed in a single column.

In both single and double column mode, if there are options that cannot be
displayed in the available space, the additional options are lost.
A menu text item that has no associated Action is treated as a sub-heading and
is displayed aligned with the option numbers.
```

### msg:6618
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6618:1#2d8049de

```
Menu Action Sentence
The Action text is the sentence to be executed for the menu option.  This may
be any sentence that could be executed from the command prompt and may include
inline prompts.

Terminate the action text with a semicolon to cause the menu processor to
issue a "Press return to continue" prompt when the command has completed.

A menu text item that has no associated Action is treated as a sub-heading and
is displayed aligned with the option numbers
```

### msg:6619
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6619:1#b12ce26d

```
Menu Option Help Text
The Help text for a menu option is displayed if the user enters the option
number followed by a question mark.  This text will be truncated if it is too
long to display on a single line.
```

### msg:6620
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6620:1#c4911bac

```
The Access Control Key
If the menu includes an access control subroutine, the subroutine is called
as the menu is prepared for display for each menu option that has an entry in
this Access line.  The value entered here is passed into the subroutine as a
key to identify the menu option being processed.  The subroutine returns a
status indicating whether the menu option is to be available or not.
```

### msg:6621
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6621:1#ce9b3fb6

```
Hiding Unavailable Options
Used with the access control subroutine, this Hide item contains a true/false
(1/0) value indicating whether unavailable items are to be hidden.  If this
item id true, unavailable items are not included in the displayed menu.  If
this value is false, non-available items are displayed with the option number
in brackets.
```

### msg:6622
> kind=message tier=reference since=- src=sdsys/messages/6622:1#f10f1585

```
The Menu Item Separator Line
Press the Return key on this line to insert a new menu item below.

Use the Kill Line action (Ctrl-K) to delete the menu item below.  Successive
use of the Kill Line operation will collect the deleted items in the kill
buffer.  These can be reinserted at a suitable position by use of the paste
(Ctrl-Y) action.
```
